Maintaining high Air, Naval, and Nuke flavors sustains our technological edge and production focus. Diplomacy and Espionage at 100 are critical for city-state alliances and spy missions to gain delegates for diplomatic victory. Gold and Science remain top priorities to fund our war effort and tech progression. Other military flavors are tuned for a multi-front defensive-offensive war against The Celts, Germany, and Ethiopia.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Ramkhamhaeng, leader of Siam (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Siam
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Naresuan's Elephant, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Suea Mop, Replacing Field Gun (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Elephant Camp, Replacing Workshop (Requires technology: Civil Service)
        - Civilization Ability: Yields, Resources, Happiness and Unit Gift frequency from Friendly and Allied City-States are doubled.
      - Leader: Ramkhamhaeng
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 8
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10

        - MinorCivHostileBias: 10
        - MinorCivWarBias: 3
        - DenounceWillingness: 8
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 3
        - Rationale: No changes to the aggressive warlike persona. Our nuclear strike on Addis Ababa successfully destroyed a large Celtic force and crippled the city, demonstrating the decisive power of our nuclear arsenal. The multi-front war remains stable: we are repelling The Celts' naval pressure, protecting city-states (though we lost Quebec City's alliance to Inca), and holding the German front. Maintaining high MinorCivFriendlyBias (10) and Diplomacy focus is crucial for our diplomatic victory through city-state alliances, which our nuke proved can drastically alter battlefield outcomes. Our alliances with Brazil and Rome remain intact. Retain competitive stance.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Lasers
        - Rationale: Lasers unlocks Nuclear Missiles, a critical upgrade to our nuclear arsenal. With The Celts leading in multiple victory types and our ongoing multi-front wars, advanced nuclear capabilities are essential for decapitation strikes against their capitals, military concentrations, and to deter their naval aggression.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Covert Action (Policy)
        - Rationale: Covert Action provides 100 Spy Points and enhances election rigging in city-states (+50% influence gain, +50% influence loss for others). This tenet synergizes with our Statecraft branch and Arsenal of Democracy, dramatically boosting our ability to secure and maintain city-state alliances, which is critical for overtaking The Celts in delegate count for diplomatic victory.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Egypt is a Declaration of Friendship partner with 4 delegates. They are at war with the Celts, making them a natural ally. Solidifying positive relations keeps them friendly and potentially supportive in the World Congress, and prevents them from aligning with our enemies.
        - UpdatedTurn: 265
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Brazil remains our defensive pact ally. It is crucial to maintain maximum positive relations to keep our alliance strong. They are fighting The Celts and can provide a valuable distraction on that front. Solidifying this friendship is vital for our security.
        - UpdatedTurn: 334
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Rome is our voluntary vassal and a Declaration of Friendship ally. Solidifying positive relations ensures continued loyalty, prevents any potential defection, and reinforces our alliance network in the face of multiple enemies. This modest boost will further align their diplomatic stance with ours.
        - UpdatedTurn: 354
        
        ### La Venta
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: La Venta remains a critical coastal city-state ally; maintaining maximum positive stance ensures continued trade route bonuses and influence, solidifying their support for our diplomatic and military objectives.
        - UpdatedTurn: 404
        
        ### Quebec City
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Quebec City is once again our ally after peace. Maximum positive relationship ensures we maintain alliance, trade routes, and city-state benefits crucial for diplomatic victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 413
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Germany remains at war with us. Maximum hostility ensures our AI continues fighting relentlessly and does not accept unfavorable peace deals.
        - UpdatedTurn: 426
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Celts remain our primary rival. They have captured Raqmu, increasing their domination progress, and continue naval attacks. Maximum hostility is essential to direct all military resources to repel their offensive and target their vulnerable coastal positions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 426
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Ethiopia is still at war with us. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI remains offensively postured against them and does not seek peace while we have naval superiority.
        - UpdatedTurn: 426
        
        ### The Inca
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Inca is a major Freedom competitor and has been actively competing for city-state alliances, as seen with Quebec City. They present a diplomatic and territorial threat. Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI remains defensively postured against them, deters further expansion, and prevents them from stealing more allied city-states crucial for our diplomatic victory. It also solidifies our stance to not accept unfavorable deals and keep focus on The Celts and Germany.
        - UpdatedTurn: 440
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Increase UseNuke to 90 to ensure aggressive nuclear strikes against The Celts' military and cities, given our atomic bomb production and upcoming nuclear missiles. Air to 100 maximizes air superiority and bomber production to support naval operations and ground attacks. Maintain UnitedNations grand strategy for diplomatic victory focus.
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - World Religion: Eastern Orthodoxy:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Siamese Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Celts:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Siamese Sphere of Influence: Panama City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: La Venta:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 439
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - German Sphere of Influence: Manila:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: The Celts
          - Repeal: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: Germany
          - Enact: Global Liberation:
            - Description: All Master-Vassal relationships are immediately and peacefully annulled.
            - Proposer: Siam

        You, Ramkhamhaeng (leader of Siam, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 444.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Ramkhamhaeng, leader of Siam (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Siam
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Naresuan's Elephant, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Suea Mop, Replacing Field Gun (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Elephant Camp, Replacing Workshop (Requires technology: Civil Service)
        - Civilization Ability: Yields, Resources, Happiness and Unit Gift frequency from Friendly and Allied City-States are doubled.
      - Leader: Ramkhamhaeng
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Lasers: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Martial Law (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * No Gold Maintenance from Garrisons.
        - * +1 Happiness and +4 Culture for each Garrison.
        - * -20% Yield penalties of Puppeted Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
        - Flavors:
          - Offense: 95
          - Mobile: 85
          - CityDefense: 90
          - UseNuke: 100
          - Growth: 60
          - Happiness: 90
      - rationale: The Celts are deploying XCOM Squads and Modern Armor into our territory via paradrops and rapid strikes, necessitating higher Mobile (85) and CityDefense (90) flavors to counter these incursions. UseNuke is increased to 100 to maximize the probability of nuclear strikes against Celtic military concentrations, cities, and vassal infrastructure, which is critical to halting their domination advance (5/8 capitals controlled). Growth is reduced to 60 as our cities are already large and war production takes priority. Happiness is raised to 90 to manage rising unhappiness in occupied frontier cities. We maintain UnitedNations grand strategy to pursue city-state alliances and diplomatic victory despite the multi-front war.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Lasers
        - Rationale: Lasers remains the top priority as it unlocks Nuclear Missiles, upgrading our atomic arsenal to counter The Celts' advanced military, destroy their capital-conquering armies, and deter their naval and air superiority. With The Celts leading in multiple victory conditions, securing next-generation nuclear weapons is essential.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Covert Action
        - Rationale: Covert Action provides 100 Spy Points and significantly enhances election-rigging in city-states (+50% influence swing), which synergizes with our Statecraft branch and Siam's doubled city-state yields. This is crucial for overtaking The Celts in delegate count for diplomatic victory and securing our alliances.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully before making any tool calls.
            
            Current State:
            - Siam (Player 6) is in the Information Era with 69 technologies.
            - We have 19 delegates (45% victory progress) vs The Celts with 22 delegates (52% victory progress).
            - We are pursuing a Diplomatic Victory (UnitedNations grand strategy).
            - We are at war with Germany, Ethiopia, and The Celts.
            - We have strong alliances with Rome (vassal), Brazil (DoF, Open Borders, trade partner), and relationships with Egypt seem okay but haven't been updated recently.
            - We have alliances with city-states: Quebec City, Hanoi, Utica, Panama City.
            - We lost some alliances: La Venta influence dropped.
            
            Key observations:
            1. The Celts are leading in multiple victory conditions:
               - Domination: They control 5 capitals (62%)
               - Science: 2 spaceship parts completed (33%)
               - Culture: Influential over 4 civs, close to 2 more
               - Diplomatic: 22 delegates vs our 19
            
            2. Our military strength is 133,241 which is actually very high compared to The Celts' 26,480. Wait, that seems extremely high. Let me check again... Yes, MilitaryStrength: 133241 for Siam vs 26480 for Celts. That's a massive advantage.
            
            3. We just built an Atomic Bomb in Nakhon Chum. We have multiple atomic bombs in production across cities.
            4. Current research: Advanced Ballistics (3 turns left).
            5. Next policy in 19 turns - we had planned Covert Action (Freedom tenet).
            6. World Congress is voting in 6 turns.
            7. We have several proposals active including Sanction The Celts, Siamese Spheres of Influence on Hanoi and Panama City, Open Door La Venta, World Ideology Order (enacted), and Scholars In Residence.
            
            Events of note:
            - Our spy was detected trying to steal from The Celts' Almaty.
            - Our carrier was killed by Germany near Bremen.
            - The Celts are using XCOM and Modern Armor to attack us near Warsaw and Ethiopia/Addis Ababa.
            - We successfully nuked Ethiopia previously and that front seems somewhat contained.
            - Our fleet is actively engaging German and Celtic naval forces.
            - We have a trade deal with Brazil for Uranium (1 Uranium for 8 GPT).
            
            Strategic reassessment:
            - We are incredibly strong militarily (133k vs 26k for Celts). This is actually dominant.
            - But The Celts are ahead diplomatically and scientifically. They have 2 spaceship parts done.
            - We need to either push for diplomatic victory fast or switch to denying Celtic victories.
            - Since The Celts are building spaceship parts, a science victory is a real threat. However, they need to launch all parts.
            - For diplomatic victory: We need 42 votes. We have 19. The Celts have 22. We need to gain 23 more delegates - that's a lot. Having Rome as a vassal gives some, but not enough. We would need massive city-state alliances.
            - The Celts are our main rival for diplomatic victory. They currently have 22 delegates. They are also allied with many city-states.
            
            Available technologies:
            - Lasers: Allows Nuclear Missile. Unlocks Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion.
            - Mobile Tactics: Allows Mechanized Infantry. Unlocks Stealth.
            - Telecommunications: Allows Interpretive Center (boosts Landmarks). Unlocks Robotics, The Internet.
            - Alternative Energy: Allows Solar Plant and Recycling Center.
            
            Our previous plan was Lasers for Nuclear Missiles. This is still good - Nuclear Missiles are devastating and we have the Uranium? Wait, looking at resources... We have 0 Uranium listed! But we just built an Atomic Bomb. Hmm, maybe we have access via trade? Brazil is giving us Uranium in deal #0 (1 Uranium for 8 GPT). So we have at least 1 Uranium from trade. But Nuclear Missiles require Uranium too.
            
            Wait, actually our resource list says Uranium: 0. But we built an atomic bomb. Let me check deals... Yes, we have a deal with Brazil: We give 8 GPT, they give 1 Uranium. That gives us access. But if that deal ends or Brazil cancels... We need to be careful. We have 25 Aluminum which is good.
            
            Actually, looking at our resources again - we have Aluminum: 25. That's plenty for spaceship parts if we wanted, but we're going diplomatic.
            
            Policy options:
            We had planned Covert Action (Freedom tenet level 1). But looking at our Freedom tree, we have Economic Union, Universal Suffrage, and Arsenal of Democracy. We haven't filled Freedom completely. Covert Action is level 1 so we can take it.
            
            However, looking at our options, maybe there are better choices. We also have Statecraft completed (6 policies), Industry (4 policies), etc.
            
            For diplomatic victory, Covert Action is actually very strong because it boosts city-state election rigging. That aligns well.
            
            Flavors assessment:
            Our current flavors are heavily military and navy focused (Naval 100, Air 100, Nuke 100, etc.) with Diplomacy 100 and Gold/Science 100.
            
            Given our massive military advantage, maybe we should be more aggressive? Or maybe we should pivot slightly more toward the diplomatic victory since the World Congress is in 6 turns.
            
            Let me think about the World Congress situation:
            - The Celts proposed International Space Station
            - Germany proposed repealing Passport System
            - Siam proposed Global Liberation (annul all vassal relationships)
            
            Global Liberation is actually bad for us because Rome is our vassal! That gives us delegates. Why did we propose that? Maybe because we thought it would hurt The Celts more (they have Germany and Ethiopia as vassals). But it would hurt us too. Actually, looking at the active resolutions, Siamese Spheres of Influence are there for Hanoi and Panama City, which gives us permanent allies. That's huge for diplomatic victory.
            
            Wait, the Celtic proposal: Enact: International Space Station. This gives science bonuses and a free Great Scientist. We should probably oppose this since they are ahead in science.
            
            The German proposal: Repeal Passport System. Passport System increases tourism modifier by 50% for all civs. The Celts are nearing cultural victory (influential over 4, close on 2 more). Repealing it would slow their cultural victory. But Germany likely proposed this because they are getting culturally overwhelmed. Actually, repealing it would REDUCE tourism, which would SLOW The Celts' cultural victory. So repealing Passport System might actually be GOOD for us because it slows Celtic cultural victory. But wait - we are also building tourism. However, cultural victory is not our primary path. Slowing down The Celts is good. But why would Germany propose this? Maybe they want to reduce Celtic tourism. Actually, as a member of World Congress, I should consider how to vote.
            
            Wait, I don't control World Congress voting directly - that's handled by AI. But I can set flavors.
            
            Let me reassess Grand Strategy:
            UnitedNations is still appropriate for diplomatic victory.
            
            Should we change research?
            Advanced Ballistics finishes in 3 turns. After that, we could go for:
            - Lasers: Nuclear Missiles require Uranium. We only have 1 Uranium from Brazil. But Nuclear Missiles are game-changing.
            - Telecommunications: Interpretive Center, leads to The Internet. The Internet doubles tourism and also has science benefits. But we're not going cultural.
            - Mobile Tactics: Mechanized Infantry. Useful for ground war.
            - Alternative Energy: Solar Plant, Recycling Center (+Aluminum).
            
            Lasers seems most aligned with our current nuclear strategy. Even with just 1 Uranium, we can build one Nuclear Missile. Actually, Nuclear Missiles consume Uranium on use? Or do they require it to build? In Civ V, building nuclear units requires Uranium. With only 1 Uranium, we can only have one nuclear missile at a time (Atomic Bomb uses 1, Nuclear Missile uses 1 or 2?). Actually in Vanilla Civ V, Atomic Bomb costs 2 Uranium, Nuclear Missile costs 2 Uranium. In Vox Populi it might be different. But we have 0 native Uranium, only 1 imported. That's very limiting.
            
            Wait, we built an Atomic Bomb. If Atomic Bomb requires Uranium and we only have 1 imported... that means either:
            1. It doesn't require Uranium in this mod, or
            2. We had some and used it, or
            3. The trade gives us enough.
            
            Regardless, Lasers still unlocks Nuclear Missiles. Even if we can only build one, it's a very powerful unit (range 12 I think? vs 10 for Atomic Bomb, and more strength). Actually in Civ V, Atomic Bombs have blast radius 2 (damage adjacent tiles too) and Nuclear Missiles have blast radius 2 but higher strength and can intercept? No, I think Nuclear Missiles in Civ V have longer range and can't be intercepted? Wait, no - I'm confusing with other units.
            
            Actually in Civ V:
            - Atomic Bomb: Range 10, Blast radius 2 tiles, kills most units, damages cities
            - Nuclear Missile: Range 12? Or range 8 global? No, that's ICBM in some mods. In Vox Populi, Nuclear Missile might be different.
            
            Let me reconsider. Maybe we should research something else. The Celts are building spaceship parts. We could try to race them scientifically? But they have 2 parts done, we have 0. That's unlikely.
            
            Alternatively, Telecommunications leads to The Internet, which doubles tourism. If The Celts get The Internet, their cultural victory accelerates massively. So we might want to beat them to Telecommunications/The Internet just to deny them.
            
            But our best path is probably still military pressure combined with diplomatic victory. Our military is massive. We should be pushing offensives.
            
            Let me look at city-state diplomacy:
            We have alliances with: Hanoi (502 influence), Utica (432), Quebec City (84), Panama City (361).
            The Celts have alliances with: Segou (516), Antananarivo (364), Ragusa (70).
            Germany has Manila (519).
            Brazil has Quebec City (84) - wait, Quebec City shows Brazil as Ally with 84. But our relations say Quebec City is Friends with us (84). Actually the city-state info says:
            - Quebec City: MajorAlly: Brazil, Brazil Ally (84), Siam Friends (84). That's a tight race.
            Wait, looking back at events: Our trade route from Sukhothai to Quebec City has 2 turns left. That means we've been influencing them.
            
            Actually, if Brazil is the ally of Quebec City with 84 influence, and we have 84 as friends... we need to make sure we win that alliance. Our spy Phailin is in Quebec City rigging elections.
            
            Our double city-state yield bonus (Siam ability) makes city-state alliances very valuable.
            
            Policy choice:
            Covert Action gives +100 Spy Points, +50% influence gain/loss when rigging elections, and +10 Network Points per turn for spies in foreign cities. This is excellent for city-state control. With our Statecraft tree complete, our spies are already very powerful. This tenet will make them even better at securing city-states.
            
            I think Covert Action is still the right choice.
            
            Research:
            After Advanced Ballistics, what next? Lasers is the planned choice. But let me consider Telecommunications. The Interpretive Center boosts Landmarks. Do we have many Landmarks? We have some Great Works. Actually, The Internet gives doubled tourism but also +25% science in the city it's built? No, in Civ V The Internet is a National Wonder that gives +1 science per 2 population and doubles tourism.
            
            Given that The Celts are at 4/6 influential and close on Brazil/Siam... wait, they are at 90% with Brazil (8 turns to influential) and 62% with us (52 turns). If they get The Internet or other tourism boosts, they could win cultural victory.
            
            To stop cultural victory, we could:
            1. Go to war with them (already are) and deny open borders/trade routes (though they have many vassals)
            2. Don't let them get The Internet
            3. Build culture domestically to raise our culture floor
            
            We have 2159 culture per turn. The Celts have 4688 tourism per turn, but our culture is probably high enough that it takes them 52 turns to influence us. But 8 turns for Brazil!
            
            If Brazil becomes culturally dominated by The Celts, that's one more civ. Then The Celts need 1 more (us or Inca). They need 6 total. Actually Cultural Victory requires influencing ALL other civs? No, in Civ V BNW, you need to be influential over all civs. Let me check... The report says "CivsNeeded: 6" and The Celts have InfluentialCivs: 4. So yes, they need all 6. Germany and Rome are already dominated, and they are close on Brazil (8 turns) and us (52 turns). So they are not imminent on us but Brazil is about to fall.
            
            If we go to Telecommunications -> The Internet, we could deny The Celts The Internet tourism boost and get it ourselves. But tourism isn't our victory path.
            
            Alternatively, Lasers gives us Nuclear Missiles. Not only are they useful for war, but they also unlock Globalization (for Diplomatic Victory? Actually in Civ V, Globalization gives an extra delegate from Diplomats. In Vox Populi, diplomats/envoys give one-time influence, but the tech description says nothing about delegates. Let me check... Globalization is not listed in the tech descriptions here. Lasers unlocks Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion.
            
            Actually for diplomatic victory in Vox Populi, you need the World Ideology elected (which has happened: Order is the World Ideology), and then you need to reach the Information Era and have the United Nations built. Then you can win by getting enough votes.
            
            Wait, the Victory Progress says:
            "DiplomaticVictory - VotesNeeded: 42 - Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns"
            "World Ideology: Order: EnactedOn: 439"
            So World Ideology Order is already enacted! That means Diplomatic Victory SHOULD be enabled if United Nations has been constructed. But wait, the description says "Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed." Has it been constructed? The report doesn't explicitly say. But if we're at 45% and 52% progress, and voting is happening... it probably has been built, or maybe it becomes available once World Ideology passes and someone builds the UN.
            
            Actually, looking at the active resolutions, I don't see United Nations as an enacted resolution. World Ideology is enacted. The UN might not be built yet. In Civ V, once World Ideology passes and someone builds the UN, the next World Congress becomes the UN and votes for World Leader.
            
            Hmm, the status says "World Congress - Voting in 6 turns". If the UN hasn't been built yet, this is just a regular congress. If it has, this could be the World Leader vote.
            
            Given the percentages (45% and 52%), it's not a World Leader vote yet because we don't have near 42 votes. So probably the UN isn't built yet, or the vote isn't happening.
            
            Wait, actually VotesNeeded: 42 might mean for World Leader. And we have 19. The Celts have 22. No one is close. So it's probably not the World Leader vote yet.
            
            Let me think differently. We need to increase our delegate count. How?
            - City-state alliances
            - World Religion (if we founded it - we didn't, we have Pantheon only)
            - World Ideology (we get +1 for following Order, +1 for each other civ following it. But most follow Freedom)
            - Diplomats/Envoys in Vox Populi might give delegates? No, in base BNW, diplomats give delegates. In Vox Populi, they changed it. Looking at Policies, Statecraft gives delegates. We have Statecraft complete so we probably get delegates from that.
            - Trading for votes? In some versions you can trade votes.
            
            Actually, looking at our Ideology: We have 3 Freedom tenets but we are listed as Order? Wait:
            - Our PolicyBranches include: Freedom (Economic Union, Universal Suffrage, Arsenal of Democracy), Industry, Rationalism, Statecraft, Imperialism, Progress, Authority.
            - We do NOT have Order listed. But our player info says under TheirOpinionToUs: "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order." Wait, looking at Brazil's opinion: "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order." No wait, that's THEIR opinion. Let me check...
            Germany: "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." That's what Germany thinks? No, the text is "OurOpinionToThem" for Germany. It says "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." Hmm, that seems backwards. Actually in Civ V diplo text, sometimes it's phrased from their perspective. Looking more carefully:
            Germany OurOpinionToThem: "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." This is Siam's opinion of Germany. Wait no - "OurOpinionToThem" means OUR opinion TO THEM. So WE think THEY adopted Order? No, Germany's policy branches show Order: 1. So Germany has 1 Order tenet. But their main ideology? They're probably Freedom. Wait, Germany has Statecraft: 6, Order: 1, Imperialism: 6, Authority: 6. They have 1 Order tenet. But the diplo modifer says "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." Hmm, maybe WE adopted Order and THEY believe in Freedom? But our policies show Freedom tenets, not Order. Actually wait - let me check our policies again:
            - Freedom: Economic Union, Universal Suffrage, Arsenal of Democracy
            Yes, we have Freedom. Arsenal of Democracy is a Freedom tenet.
            
            But Germany's opinion says "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." This might be a bug in the display or I'm misreading. Actually looking at The Celts: "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order." Wait no - The Celts OurOpinionToThem: "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." But The Celts' policies show Order: 11. So The Celts have Order. The text might mean "You [The Celts] have chosen to adopt Order, but we [Siam] believe in Freedom." Yes! That makes sense. It's written from the perspective of the reporter, describing the target. So The Celts believe in Order, and we (Siam) believe in Freedom.
            
            Wait, but then Germany's says: "You have adopted the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." If this is our opinion of Germany, it would mean Germany adopted Order... but Germany's policies show Order: 1. Actually maybe they adopted Order too? Let me look at Germany again... Germany has Statecraft: 6, Order: 1, Imperialism: 6, Authority: 6. Just 1 Order tenet. That's probably not their ideology.
            
            Actually, the diplo modifier might be about us. Let me re-read the format.
            The section is "OurOpinionToThem" under Player info for each player. This lists reasons for OUR attitude toward THEM.
            For The Celts: "You have chosen to adopt the Freedom Ideology, but they believe in Order." Wait, this still confuses me.
            
            Let me look at The Inca's OurOpinionToThem: "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Freedom." The Inca has Order: 3. So The Inca has some Order. But they also have Tradition, Artistry, Statecraft, Rationalism, Progress. If they are Freedom, then "You [The Inca] have adopted Freedom, but we [Siam] believe in Order" - but we don't believe in Order, we have Freedom.
            
            I think there might be some confusion in the text which could be bugged or from a different perspective. But the key is: World Ideology Order was enacted by someone. We voted for it? Or it was proposed and passed. Since we have Freedom tenets, we probably voted against it or didn't care. But it's enacted now.
            
            Actually, looking at the active resolutions: "World Ideology: Order: EnactedOn: 439" - it passed 5 turns ago. Probably The Celts got it passed.
            
            Anyway, back to strategy.
            
            Our current war situations:
            - Germany: War, our score +32%, war weariness 17%. We're winning. Their city Bremen is at 17% health.
            - Ethiopia: War, our score +100%, war weariness 18%. Total domination.
            - The Celts: War, our score -6%, war weariness 22%. We're slightly losing the tactical score but our overall military is much stronger.
            - The Inca: Denounced, not at war but hostile.
            
            Our tactical posture shows:
            - We're steamrolling near Bremen (16911) with massive naval superiority in that zone.
            - We're pushing on Salvador (14110) with steamroll posture.
            - Even near Warsaw (18949) which has enemy superiority, we're holding.
            - The Celts have advanced units: Modern Armor, Mechanized Infantry, XCOM, Stealth Bombers, Jet Fighters.
            - We have lots of Fleet Destroyers, Infantry, Anti-Aircraft Guns, Fighters, and Atomic Bombs.
            
            Given our massive military advantage (133k vs 26k), we should be able to crush The Celts if we focus properly. The issue is they have a huge empire (27 cities) and many wonders.
            
            For our flavors, we should maintain:
            - Nuke: 100
            - UseNuke: 90
            - Air: 100
            - Offense: 90
            - Naval: 100
            - NavalRecon: 100
            - Diplomacy: 100
            - Gold: 100
            - Science: 100
            - Production: 95
            - Defense: 85
            - Mobilization: 90
            
            But maybe we should tone down Naval from 100 since we already have naval superiority? Actually, The Celts still have naval forces attacking. Let's keep Naval high.
            
            We should reduce Espionage from 100? Spies are important for city-states... Actually, our spies are currently:
            - 1 in Quebec City rigging elections
            - 1 in Inca city gathering intel
            - 1 in Celtic city gathering intel
            - 1 killed
            - Others in various places.
            
            The Covert Action policy would help tremendously with city-state rigging. Let's keep that plan.
            
            Wait, looking at the policy list again... We don't HAVE to take Covert Action. We also have available:
            - Draft Registration (Freedom L1): Military units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
            - Creative Expression (Freedom L1): +2 Tourism from Great Works, +3 GAP and Culture from culture buildings.
            - Civil Society (Freedom L1): Specialists consume 2 less food. Farms etc produce +4 food.
            - Avant Garde (Freedom L1): +33% Great People birth rate. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom.
            
            Given our diplomatic focus, Covert Action is clearly best.
            
            For research, Lasers is still good. It leads to Globalization which in BNW gives +1 delegate from diplomats... but in Vox Populi diplomats were replaced by Great Diplomats which give influence. Actually, the tech tree description for Lasers says: "Completing it would unlock: Particle Physics, Globalization, Nuclear Fusion". The description doesn't mention delegates. But in base BNW, Globalization gives +1 vote from Diplomats. In Vox Populi, I don't know if that changed.
            
            Actually, looking at our technologies: 69. The Celts have 80. The Inca have 75. We are behind. Scholars In Residence is enacted, giving us -20% research cost for known techs, up to -50% with city-state allies. We should leverage this to catch up.
            
            Telecommunications also leads to Robotics (same as Alternative Energy) and The Internet. The Internet is a wonder that doubles tourism. Not critical for us.
            
            Mobile Tactics leads to Stealth. Stealth Bombers are devastating.
            
            I think Lasers is still best for the nuclear option and potentially Globalization.
            
            Let me reconsider whether we should try for Science Victory instead. We have 0 parts. The Celts have 2 parts. That's a huge gap. Diplomatic might be easier.
            
            Alternatively, Domination? We have 11 cities, The Celts have 27. We would need to capture capitals. Our military is very strong though.
            
            Actually, Siam's unique ability doubles yields from friendly/allied city-states. This makes diplomatic victory incredibly powerful for us economically. We should stick with Diplomatic.
            
            For the immediate World Congress in 6 turns, we should consider how to vote. The proposals are:
            1. Enact International Space Station (The Celts) - BAD for us, helps their science victory.
            2. Repeal Passport System (Germany) - Good for slowing Celtic tourism, but they probably won't win cultural anytime soon against us.
            3. Enact Global Liberation (Siam) - Annuls vassals. BAD for us since Rome is our vassal. Wait, why did we propose this? Maybe it was proposed before we had Rome as vassal? Or maybe we think it hurts The Celts more? The Celts have Germany and Ethiopia as vassals, which gives them votes. Annuling those would reduce their delegate count significantly! And we only lose Rome (our vassal). If The Celts lose 2 vassals, that's probably 2-3 delegates each? Actually vassals might give delegates to the master. If so, this could reduce The Celts' delegates from 22 to ~16-17, while we drop from 19 to ~17. That would be a net positive for us relative to them! That might be why we proposed it.
            
            Wait, but would Global Liberation pass? The Celts and their vassals would vote against it. But we, our allies, and maybe Inca/Brazil would vote for it.
            
            Actually, vassalage in Civ V BNW: A vassal gives their master extra votes in the World Congress based on city count or population? I'm not entirely sure of Vox Populi mechanics. But if it denies The Celts Germany and Ethiopia's votes, it's a brilliant move.
            
            However, looking at the active resolutions, we already have Sanction The Celts and Passport System enacted. Global Liberation would be a bold play.
            
            Given our massive military, we could also pivot to Conquest and just take Celtic capitals. But they have 5 capitals spread across their empire.
            
            Let me reconsider the research path. If Global Liberation is our main short-term diplomatic goal, it doesn't require specific techs. But Nuclear Missiles could be used to destroy Celtic armies and cities, weakening them.
            
            I think we should:
            1. Maintain UnitedNations grand strategy
            2. Keep flavors heavy on military and diplomacy
            3. Research Lasers next for nuclear missiles
            4. Take Covert Action next policy
            5. Keep relationships as they are (hostile to enemies, friendly to allies)
            6. Maybe slightly adjust relationship with Egypt? They haven't been updated in a while but they're DoF partners.
            
            Actually, looking at players, Egypt was defeated? Wait, under Players it says:
            "7: Defeated Major Civilization Egypt"
            Egypt has been eliminated! That means The Celts captured Thebes (which they did, it's shown as occupied). So Egypt is gone.
            
            Wait, but earlier it said Egypt is our DoF partner with 4 delegates. But now "Defeated Major Civilization Egypt" implies they are out. However they still appear in the relationships list. Maybe they were resurrected? Or maybe they are just defeated but exist as a minor? Actually, in Civ V when a civ is defeated they're gone. The delegate count might be outdated. Looking at Victory Progress Diplomatic section: Egypt is not even listed! Listed are Brazil (15), Ethiopia (15), Germany (13), Rome (6), Siam (19), The Celts (22), The Inca (12). No Egypt. So Egypt is indeed gone. Their entry in relationships might be stale.
            
            Given this, we don't need to worry about Egypt.
            
            Wait, there's a stale relationship entry for Egypt that says "UpdatedTurn: 265". That's very old. We should probably remove it by setting it to 0, but the tool says we only need to change when necessary. It's harmless.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully at our situation: We are the #2 score (5646) behind The Celts (5386). Wait, 5646 > 5386. We actually have a HIGHER score than The Celts! The Celts are ahead in most victories but we have higher overall score.
            
            Given that we're in a strong position score-wise and militarily, but need to close the diplomatic gap... 
            
            One critical thing: Our war weariness with The Celts is 22% and our score is -6%. We're slightly losing the tactical exchange despite our massive overall military. This is because The Celts have better tech (Information Era with 80 techs vs our 69) and units like Stealth Bombers and XCOM. We need to leverage our nukes more effectively.
            
            Our UseNuke is at 90. Maybe we should max it at 100 to ensure the AI uses atomic bombs we have. We have at least one atomic bomb ready (ID 19402 built in Nakhon Chum, plus ID 19333 rebased to Nakhon Chum). We should be dropping these on Celtic armies or cities.
            
            Let me check flavors. We had UseNuke: 90. Let's push it to 100.
            
            Also, Antiair is at 100 but The Celts are using air power. We should keep that. We bought AA guns this turn.
            
            Espionage is at 100. Spies are doing city-state work and intelligence. That's fine.
            
            What about Culture flavor? It's at 40. With The Celts nearing cultural victory (though not imminent on us), should we raise culture? Actually, if we increase our culture, it takes longer for them to influence us. But 52 turns is a long time. We have higher priorities.
